\sin\angle(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)})={\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q-1)},\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q+1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\over\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})},

and

\sin\angle(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(q)})={\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q-1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)}),\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q+1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\over\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})},

We shall derive lower bounds for the nominators and an upper bound for the denominator. It suffices to consider the case
q=1
. Other indices can be treated in an identical fashion.

B.1
Lower Bound for
\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

Denote by
\tilde{\Sigma}=\Sigma-\sigma_{0}^{2}\mathscr{I}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

(19)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max_{l\notin\pi([k-1])}\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max_{l\notin\pi([k-1])}(\Sigma-\sigma_{0}^{2}\mathscr{I})\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)})

\displaystyle-\sup_{\|\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|\leq 1,1\leq q\leq p}\left|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})\right|.

Next we bound the two terms on the rightmost hand side.

Starting with the first term, note that for any
l\notin\pi([k-1])
,

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\sigma_{0}^{2}\mathscr{I})(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq l^{\prime}\leq r}\left[\sigma_{l^{\prime}}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(q)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{l^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(q)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\|\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(q)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\|^{4}.

By the induction hypothesis (
17
),

\|\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(q)}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\|^{2}=\sum_{1\leq l^{\prime}<k}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{l^{\prime}}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\leq\sum_{1\leq l^{\prime}<k}\tilde{\delta}^{2}_{l^{\prime}}\leq C\delta_{k}^{2}.

By taking the constant
c_{0}
of (
10
) small enough, we can ensure that

\max_{l\notin\pi([k-1])}(\Sigma-\sigma_{0}^{2}\mathscr{I})(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(p)})\geq(1-C\delta_{k}^{4})\tau^{2}.

(20)

where

\tau^{2}=\max_{l\notin\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}.

Next we derive a bound for

\sup_{\|\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|\leq 1,1\leq q\leq p}(\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(p)}).

Note that

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(p)}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})

(21)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,l_{1}l_{2}}-\Sigma_{\theta,l_{1}l_{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})]^{2}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\|\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|^{2}\right).

We bound the three terms on the right hand side separately.

The first term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\biggl|\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il_{1}}\theta_{il_{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I_{r}\|\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I_{r}\|\left[\sum_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}+\sum_{l\notin\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}\right],

Recall that for any
l\in\pi([k-1])
,

|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle|=|\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle|\leq\|\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\|\leq\delta_{l}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l<k}\{\sigma_{\pi(l)}^{2}\delta_{l}^{2p-2}\}\leq\max_{1\leq l<k}\{\sigma_{\pi(l)}^{2}\delta_{l}^{2}\}\leq\tau^{2},

by taking
c_{0}
of (
10
) small enough. On the other hand,

\sum_{l\notin\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}\leq\max_{l\notin\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}=\tau^{2}.

This implies that

\left|\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,l_{1}l_{2}}-\Sigma_{\theta,l_{1}l_{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right|\leq C\tau^{2}\sqrt{r\over n}.

(22)

Similarly, the second term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})\right|

(23)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\tau\sigma_{0}\sqrt{d\over n}.

Finally, the third term can be bounded by

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})]^{2}-\sigma_{0}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\|\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|^{2}\right|\leq\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathscr{E}}-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}}\|\leq C\sigma_{0}^{2}\sqrt{d\over n}.

(24)

Combing (
21
)-(
24
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|\leq 1,1\leq q\leq p}\left|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\tau^{2}\sqrt{r\over n}+C(\sigma_{0}\tau+\sigma_{0}^{2})\sqrt{d\over n}.

Together with (
19
), this implies

\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\geq\tau^{2}\left(1-C\delta_{k}^{4}-C\sqrt{r\over n}\right)-C(\sigma_{0}\tau+\sigma_{0}^{2})\sqrt{d\over n}-C\sigma_{0}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2},

(25)

by taking
c_{0}
of (
10
) small enough.

B.2
Upper Bounds for
\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}),\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

Observe that for any
\mathbf{w}
orthogonal to
\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

(26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\theta_{il}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il_{1}}\theta_{il_{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\sigma_{0}^{2}\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)}\rangle.

Again each term on the right hand side needs to be bounded carefully.

The first term on the right hand side of (
26
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{2}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}\|_{\max}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}\mathbf{w}\rangle|.

In particular, when
\mathbf{w}=\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle(1-\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1-\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\|^{2}\leq\sum_{1\leq l<k}\tilde{\delta}_{l}^{2}\leq C\delta_{k}^{2},

by taking
c_{0}
small enough. Thus,

\left|\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\right|\leq C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}.

(27)

The second term on the right hand side of (
26
) can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\theta_{il}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}\max_{l\neq\pi(k)}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle|\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau\delta_{k}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\max_{l\neq\pi(k)}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\delta_{k}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\max\left\{\max_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle|\right\},\max_{l\notin\pi([k])}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle|\right\}\right\}.

Note that

\max_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle|\right\}\leq\max_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(q)}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle|\right\}\leq\max_{1\leq l<k}\{\sigma_{\pi(l)}\delta_{l}^{p-1}\}\leq 2\tau\delta_{k}^{p-1},

and

\max_{l\notin\pi([k])}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle|\right\}\leq\tau\delta_{k}^{p-1}.

We get

\left|\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\theta_{il}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right|\leq C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p}\sqrt{r\over n}.

(28)

And similarly, when
\mathbf{w}=\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}
, we bound the third term on the right hand side of (
26
) by

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\right|

(29)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}\max_{l\neq\pi(k)}\left\{\sigma_{l}\prod_{q=2}^{p}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p}\sqrt{r\over n},

where in the last inequality we used the fact that

\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle^{2}\leq\|\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}\leq\delta_{k}^{2};

the fourth term by

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il_{1}}\theta_{il_{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\right|

(30)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2(p-1)};

the fifth term by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\right]

(31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\left\{\sigma_{l}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle\left[{1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\cdot\left[\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\left(\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma_{0}\tau\delta_{k}^{p-1}\sqrt{d\over n};

and the sixth term by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]

(32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{\pi(k)}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|+\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma_{0}\tau\sqrt{d\over n}.

Finally the last term can be bounded by

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\sigma_{0}^{2}\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}\rangle\right|\leq\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathscr{E}}-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}}\|\leq C\sigma_{0}^{2}\sqrt{d\over n}.

Together with (
27
)-(
32
), we get

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}),\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\leq C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}+C(\sigma_{0}^{2}+\sigma_{0}\tau)\sqrt{d\over n}.

B.3
Upper Bounds for
\max_{l\notin\pi([k])}\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

To derive the helper bound (
17
), we also need an upper bound for

\max_{l\notin\pi([k])}\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)}).

We shall follow a similar step by bound each term on the right hand side of (
26
), but now with
\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}
(
m\notin\pi([k])
).

Specifically, the first term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{2}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}\|_{\max}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle|.

Note that

\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle=0.

We get

|\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle|=|\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle|\leq\|\widehat{{\cal P}}\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\|\|\widehat{{\cal P}}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\|\leq\sum_{1\leq l<k}\tilde{\delta}_{l}^{2}\leq C\delta_{k}^{2},

by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. This implies that

\left|\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|\leq C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}.

by taking
c_{0}
small enough.

The second term can also be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\theta_{il}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\||\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p+1}\sqrt{r\over n}.

We bound the third term by

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{m}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{im}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\sum_{l\notin\{\pi(k),m\}}\sigma_{l}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

The first term on the right hand side can be further bounded by

\tau^{2}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-\Sigma_{\theta}\|_{\max}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\left|\langle\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right|\leq C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p-1}\sqrt{\log r\over n}.

Now consider the second term:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l\notin\{\pi(k),m\}}\sigma_{l}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\left(\sum_{l\notin\{\pi(k),m\}}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}-I\|\Biggl(\sum_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+\sum_{l\notin\pi([k])\cup\{m\}}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\Biggr)^{1/2}.

The first term in the bracket on the rightmost hand side can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\|\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\|^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(2)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)}\rangle^{2}\right)\left(\max_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\|\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\|^{2}\prod_{q=3}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta_{k}^{2}\left(\max_{l\in\pi([k-1])}\sigma_{l}^{2}\delta_{l}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2(p-2)}\right)\leq C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2p};

the second term by

\|\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\|^{2}\left(\max_{l\notin\pi([k])\cup\{m\}}\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)\leq C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2p},

so that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\sigma_{\pi(k)}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p-1}\sqrt{\log r\over n}+\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p}\sqrt{r\over n}\right).

Similar to before, the fourth term on the right hand side of (
26
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il_{1}}\theta_{il_{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta}\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2(p-1)};

and the fifth term by

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\left\{\sigma_{l}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle\left[{1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{il}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\right]\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\cdot\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma_{0}\tau\delta_{k}^{p-1}\sqrt{d\over n}.

We now turn to the sixth term on the right hand side of (
26
). Write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{\pi(k)}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right].

The first term again can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\left(\sum_{l\neq\pi(k)}\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\sigma_{0}\tau\delta_{k}^{p-1}\sqrt{d\over n}.

For the second term, note that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{\pi(k)}\theta_{i\pi(k)}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]\right|

(33)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|+\tau\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|,

where the second inequality follows from triangular inequality. As before,

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|\leq\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\|\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\|\leq C\sigma_{0}\delta_{k}\sqrt{d\over n}.

To bound the first term on the rightmost hand side of (
33
), write

\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}=\alpha_{q}\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}+\mathbf{v}^{(q)}

where
\alpha_{q}=\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}\notin\{\pi(k),m\}\notin\{\pi(k),m\}\notin\{\pi(k),m\}^{(q)}\rangle
and
\|\mathbf{v}^{(q)}\|=\sqrt{1-\alpha_{q}^{2}}\leq\delta_{k}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{p}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})+\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{v}^{(p)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{p}\alpha_{p-1}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-2)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})

\displaystyle+\alpha_{p}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-2)},\mathbf{v}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})

\displaystyle+\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{v}^{(p)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cdots\cdots

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\alpha_{q}\right)\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})

\displaystyle+\cdots+\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{v}^{(p)}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|{1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|+\cdots+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{v}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|{1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})\theta_{i\pi(k)}\right]\right|+C_{p}\delta_{k}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\sigma_{0}\sqrt{\log r\over n}+\sigma_{0}\delta_{k}\sqrt{d\over n}\right),

so that the six term on the rightmost hand side of (
26
) can be upper bounded by

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\sigma_{l}\theta_{il}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right)\right]\right|\leq C\left(\sigma_{0}\tau\sqrt{\log r\over n}+\sigma_{0}\tau\delta_{k}\sqrt{d\over n}\right).

Finally consider the seventh term:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\langle\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}\rangle\right|.

Similar to before, the second term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\sigma_{0}^{2}\langle\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\cal P}}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)}\|\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathscr{E}}-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}}\|\leq C\sigma_{0}^{2}\delta_{k}\sqrt{d\over n};

the first term by

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\sigma_{0}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{v}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\sigma_{0}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}^{(1)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{k}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathscr{E}}-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}}\|+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\cdots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\cdots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})\right|

\displaystyle+\cdots+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p-1)},\mathbf{v}^{(p)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\sigma_{0}^{2}\delta_{k}\sqrt{d\over n}+\sigma_{0}^{2}\sqrt{\log r\over n}\right).

Putting all seven upper bounds together, we have

\max_{l\notin\pi([k])}\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\leq C\left(\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}+(\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p-1}+\sigma_{0}\tau+\sigma_{0}^{2})\sqrt{\log r\over n}\right).

B.4
Finishing Up

We first verify (
18
). Note that

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}})(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{r}\left(\sigma_{l}^{2}\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq r}\left\{\sigma_{l}^{2}\left|\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right|\right\}\cdot\left(\sum_{l=1}^{r}\left|\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq r}\left\{\sigma_{l}^{2}\left|\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(q)}\rangle\right|\right\}.

where the last inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. Therefore, by definition,

\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}\geq(\Sigma-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}})(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

(34)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}})(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

\displaystyle-\sup_{\|\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|\leq 1,1\leq q\leq p}\left|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(p)})

\displaystyle-\sup_{\|\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|\leq 1,1\leq q\leq p}\left|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}})(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{u}_{l_{\ast}}^{(p)})

\displaystyle-2\sup_{\|\mathbf{w}^{(q)}\|\leq 1,1\leq q\leq p}\left|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)},\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{w}^{(1)},\dots,\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(p)}\mathbf{w}^{(p)})\right|

where
l_{\ast}
is the index such that
\sigma_{l_{\ast}}^{2}=\tau^{2}
. Following the same derivation as before, we have

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\Sigma_{\mathscr{E}})(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})\geq\tau^{2}\left(1-C\sqrt{r\over n}-C\delta_{k}\right).

Together with (
34
), we get

\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}\geq\tau^{2}\left(1-C\sqrt{r\over n}-C\delta_{k}\right).

Combing the lower bound for
\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})
and upper bound for
\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}),\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})
, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{C\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}+C(\sigma_{0}^{2}+\sigma_{0}\tau)\sqrt{d\over n}\over\tau^{2}\left(1-C\delta_{k}^{4}-C\sqrt{r\over n}\right)-C(\sigma_{0}\tau+\sigma_{0}^{2})\sqrt{d\over n}-C\sigma_{0}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{k}^{2}+C\left({\sigma_{0}\over\tau}+{\sigma_{0}^{2}\over\tau^{2}}\right)\sqrt{d\over n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left({\sigma_{0}\over\sigma_{\pi(k)}}+{\sigma_{0}^{2}\over\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}}\right)\sqrt{d\over n}=\delta_{k}.

Similarly, Combing the lower bound for
\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})
and upper bound for
\max_{l\notin\pi([k])}\tilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{\perp}^{(1)}\mathbf{u}_{m}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})
, we get

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{C\left(\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}+(\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{p-1}+\sigma_{0}\tau+\sigma_{0}^{2})\sqrt{\log r\over n}\right)\over\tau^{2}\left(1-C\delta_{k}^{4}-C\sqrt{r\over n}\right)-C(\sigma_{0}\tau+\sigma_{0}^{2})\sqrt{d\over n}-C\sigma_{0}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{k}^{2}+C\left(\delta_{k}+{\sigma_{0}\over\tau}+{\sigma_{0}^{2}\over\tau^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\log r\over n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta_{k}^{2}+C\left({\sigma_{0}\over\sigma_{\pi(k)}}+{\sigma_{0}^{2}\over\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\log r\over n}=\tilde{\delta}_{k}.

Appendix C
Proof of Theorems
4.1
and
4.2

Note that Theorem
4.1
can be viewed as special case of Theorem
4.2
and it suffices to prove Theorem
4.2
. As before, we only need to consider the case when
q=1
. Write

\mathbf{w}=\langle\mathbf{w},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}+\tilde{\mathbf{w}}.

Then

\langle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{w}\rangle-\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\mathbf{w}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\left(\langle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle-1\right)+\langle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\rangle.

Under the assumption
d=o(n^{1/2})
, by Lemma
1
and Theorem
3.2
, it is not hard to see that

\displaystyle\sin\angle(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)})=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right),

(35)

so

1-\langle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle\leq 1-\langle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}=\sin^{2}\angle(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)})=O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Therefore it suffices to prove that

\sqrt{n}\left(\langle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\rangle-\langle\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\rangle\right)\to_{d}N\left(0,\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}\left({\sigma_{0}^{2}\over\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}}+{\sigma_{0}^{4}\over\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{4}}\right)\right).

Recall that
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}
is the leading eigenvector of

\widehat{\Sigma}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)}),

which is the same as the leading eigenvector of

\tilde{\Sigma}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)}),

so

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)}=\frac{\tilde{\Sigma}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})}{\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})},

which implies

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\rangle=\frac{\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{w}},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})}{\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})}.

We start with the nominator. Following an identical argument as that for (
26
) in Subsection
B.3
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{w}},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})-\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})

\displaystyle\leq\

\displaystyle C\left(\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2}+\tau^{2}\delta^{p+1}\sqrt{r\over n}+\tau^{2}\delta^{p-1}\sqrt{r\over n}+\tau^{2}\delta_{k}^{2(p-1)}+\sigma_{0}\tau\delta_{k}\sqrt{d\over n}\right)=o(n^{-1/2})

with probability tending to one.
So

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{w}},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})+o_{p}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pi(k)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{\pi(k)i}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})-\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}+o_{p}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\to_{d}

\displaystyle N(0,(\sigma_{0}^{4}+\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2})\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}).

On the other hand, similar to the previous section,

\tilde{\Sigma}(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)},\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(2)},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{k}^{(p)})=\sigma_{\pi(k)}^{2}(1+o_{p}(1)).

Theorem
4.2
then follows from Slutsky’s Theorem.

The claim in Theorem
4.1
that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left[{\rm vec}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{(q)})-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{U}^{(q)}_{\pi})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\to}N\left(0,{\rm diag}\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{\pi(1)}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{\pi(1)}^{4}}\right){\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{\pi(1)}^{(q)}}^{\perp},\ldots,\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{\pi(r)}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{\pi(r)}^{4}}\right){\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{\pi(r)}^{(q)}}^{\perp}\right)\right)

follows from Theorem
4.2
and the fact that

\sigma_{\pi(k)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{\pi(k)i}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{i}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(p)})-\mathbf{u}_{\pi(k)}^{(1)}

are independent for any
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorems
4.3
and
4.4

Theorem
4.3
is a special case of Theorem
4.4
and it suffices to prove the latter. We first need to introduce a number of notations. Denote

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\cdot):=\mathbb{P}(\cdot|\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}),

(36)

and

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}(\cdot):=\mathbb{E}(\cdot|\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}),

(37)

the conditional probability and expectation given
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\},
respectively.

Write

\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(q)[1]}:=\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[1]}\otimes\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[1]},\qquad{\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}=\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}={\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}},

{\cal C}_{j}^{(q)}:=\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left(I_{d_{q}}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\right),

\displaystyle z_{jk}:=\mathscr{X}_{k}\times_{1}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)[2]}\dots\times_{q-1}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q-1)[2]}\times_{q+1}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q+1)[2]}\dots\times_{p}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\qquad k=1,\dots,n/2,

and

\displaystyle M_{j}^{(q)[1]}:=\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q^{\prime}\neq q}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q^{\prime})},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q^{\prime})[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}+I_{d_{q}}=\sum_{l=1}^{r}\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}+I_{d_{q}},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}=\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q^{\prime}\neq q}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q^{\prime})},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q^{\prime})[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}.

Furthermore, let

\displaystyle{\cal C}_{j}^{(q)[1]}=\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}}\left(I_{d_{q}}-\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\right),

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{j}^{(q)[1]}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left({\cal C}^{(q)[1]}z_{jk}\otimes{\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}z_{jk}+{\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}z_{jk}\otimes{\cal C}^{(q)[1]}z_{jk}\right),

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{j}^{(q)[1]}=\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(q)[1]}-{\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}-{\cal L}_{j}^{(q)[1]}.

We use calligraphic capital letters for
{\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}
,
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(q)[1]}
,
{\cal C}_{j}^{(q)}
,
{\cal C}_{j}^{(q)[1]}
,
{\cal L}_{j}^{(q)[1]}
and
{\cal S}_{j}^{(q)[1]}
to remind the readers that they are matrices with specific definitions.

Define

\displaystyle b_{j}^{(q)[1]}:=\left\langle\bar{\mathbb{E}}({\cal S}_{j}^{(q)[1]})\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\right\rangle.

(38)

and
b_{j}^{(q)[2]}
is similarly defined. Finally, we define

\displaystyle b_{j}^{(q)}=\frac{\left\|\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[1]}+\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[2]}\right\|}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(q)[1]}}+\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(q)[2]}}}-1..

(39)

The proof is rather involved and we shall break it into several steps.

Step 1.

We shall represent linear forms of

\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[1]}
as bilinear forms of
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(q)[1]}
, and prove that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left((1+b_{j}^{(q)})\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}^{(q)}_{j},\mathbf{v}\rangle-\langle\mathbf{u}^{(q)}_{\pi(j)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right)\to_{d}N\left(0,\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{\pi(j)}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{\pi(j)}^{4}}\right)\|{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{\pi(j)}^{(q)}}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}\|^{2}\right).

(40)

Step 2.

We prove that

\displaystyle\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(j)}^{(q)}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{d_{q}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{\pi(j)}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{\pi(j)}^{4}}\right)}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d^{3/2}}{n^{3/2}}\right).

(41)

Notice that by letting
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{u}^{(q)}_{\pi(j)}
, (
40
) implies

\displaystyle(1+b_{j}^{(q)})\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{\pi(j)}^{(q)}\rangle-1=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Combine it with (
41
), we immediately have

\displaystyle b_{j}^{(q)}=\sqrt{1+\frac{d_{q}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{\pi(j)}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{\pi(j)}^{4}}\right)}-1+O_{p}\left(\frac{d^{3/2}}{n^{3/2}}\right)+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

Step 3.

Finally, we show that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{b}_{j}^{(q)}-b_{j}^{(q)}\right)\overset{p}{\to}0.

(42)

For simplicity, in the rest of the proof we shall assume without loss of generality that the permutation
\pi
that matches
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)}
to
\mathbf{u}_{\pi(j)}^{(1)}
is the identity. We shall also make repeated use of the following facts, oftentimes without explicit mentioning.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
3.2
, write

\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathscr{E}}^{[1]}={2\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{i}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{i},\qquad\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}^{[1]}={2\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n/2}\theta_{i}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{i}.

By Lemmas
1
and
2
, with probability tending to one,

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{-2}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathscr{E}}^{[1]}-\mathscr{I}\|\leq C\sqrt{d\over n},\ \sigma_{0}^{-1}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}^{[1]}\|\leq C\sqrt{d\over n}.

(43)

Let

\Delta^{(q),[2]}=\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}.

By Theorem
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{q\in[p]}\|\Delta^{(q),[2]}\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)

Moreover, under the assumption
d=o(n)
, by Lemma
1
and Theorem
3.2
, it is not hard to see that

\displaystyle\sin\angle(\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)})=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right).

Denote

\delta^{(1)[2]}:=\max\left\{\max_{q\in[p]}\|\Delta^{(q),[2]}\|,\|\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)[1]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\|\right\},

combine the two bounds above, we have that under the assumptions for Theorem
4.4
,

\displaystyle\delta^{(1)[2]}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right).

(44)

D.1
Step 1.

Without loss of generality, for this step we assume
\sigma_{0}=1
. We only need to prove for the case
q=1
, so within this step, we shall also write
{\cal P}_{j}={\cal P}_{j}^{(1)}={\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}
and
{\cal C}_{j}={\cal C}_{j}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left(I-{\cal P}_{j}\right)
for simplicity.

Define

\displaystyle\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v}):=\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle,

where

\displaystyle y_{jk}:=\mathscr{X}_{k}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}=\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}+\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}.

Recall that
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(1)[1]}:=\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\otimes\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}
and
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(1)[2]}:=\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\otimes\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}
. Define

\displaystyle\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[1]}

\displaystyle:=\left\langle((\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(1)[1]}-(1+b_{j}^{(1)[1]}){\cal P}_{j})\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\right\rangle,\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{H}.

(45)

\displaystyle\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[2]}

\displaystyle:=\left\langle((\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{[2]}-(1+b_{j}^{(1)[2]}){\cal P}_{j})\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\right\rangle,\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{H}.

(46)

Equation (6.6) in
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2014
provides the representation of linear forms of
\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}
in terms of
\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[1]}
and
\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[2]}
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}\left[\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}+\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}-\left(\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[1]}}+\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[2]}}\right)\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\right],\mathbf{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{2}\sqrt{n}\bigg[\frac{\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[h]}}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[h]}+\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[h]}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[h]}}}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[h]}+\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[h]}}(\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[h]}+\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[h]}}+\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[h]}})}\rho_{j}(u_{j}^{(1)})^{[h]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle\bigg].

(47)

We shall make use of the following lemma:

Lemma 3
.

Under the same assumptions in Theorem
4.4
,
for any
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{q}}
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\bigg\langle\left[\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(q)[1]}-(1+b_{j}^{(q)[1]}){\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}\right]\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-{\sqrt{2\over n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle y_{jk}^{(q)},{\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk}^{(q)},{\cal C}_{j}^{(q)}\mathbf{u}\rangle+\langle y_{jk}^{(q)},{\cal P}_{j}^{(q)}\mathbf{u}\rangle\langle y_{jk}^{(q)},{\cal C}_{j}^{(q)}\mathbf{v}\rangle\right]=o_{p}\left(\|\mathbf{u}\|\|\mathbf{v}\|\right).

Further more, there exists universal constant
C
such that
\mathbb{P}(|b_{j}^{(q)[1]}|\leq C{d}/{n})\to 1
as
n\to\infty
.

Following from Lemma
3
, observe that
{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[1]}-\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n}{2}}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})\overset{p}{\to}0,

(48)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\rho_{j}(\mathbf{v})^{[2]}-\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sum_{k=\frac{n}{2}+1}^{n}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})\overset{p}{\to}0.

(49)

If we can show that for any
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})\overset{d}{\to}N\left(0,\frac{1+\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\|{\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}\|^{2}\right),

(50)

then combining with the facts that
b_{j}^{(1)[h]}\overset{p}{\to}0
and (
47
), (
48
) and (
49
), we have:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}\left[\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}+\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}-\left(\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[1]}}+\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[2]}}\right)\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\right],\mathbf{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\to}

\displaystyle N\left(0,\frac{1+\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\|{\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}\|^{2}\right).

Recall that
b_{j}^{(1)[h]}\overset{p}{\to}0
. By Slutsky’s Theorem,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left((1+b_{j}^{(1)})\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}^{(1)}_{j},\mathbf{v}\rangle-\langle\mathbf{u}^{(1)}_{j},\mathbf{v}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[1]}}+\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[2]}}}\cdot\sqrt{n}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}\left[\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}+\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}-\left(\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[1]}}+\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[2]}}\right)\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\right],\mathbf{v}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\to}

\displaystyle N\left(0,\frac{1+\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\|{\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}\|^{2}\right).

The claim (
40
) then follows.

We shall now prove (
50
). Note that
{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}=\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}
, and
{\cal C}_{j}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}
, we have

\displaystyle\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})=\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right)\left(\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}({\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v})\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right).

Both
\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}
and
\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}(\mathbb{I}_{d_{1}}{\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v})\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}
are centered Gaussian random variables. Moreover, they are independent since
\theta_{jk}
is independent with
\mathscr{E}_{k}
, and
\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\perp{\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}
. So

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})=0.

More generally, Gaussian variable
\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}
and Gaussian vector
\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}({\cal P}_{j}^{\perp})\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}
are independent for the exact same reason. So direct calculation gives:

\displaystyle{\rm var}(\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\mathbb{E}\left(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left(\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}({\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v})\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1+\sigma_{j}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\|{\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}\|^{2}.

With the fact that
\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})
are i.i.d for
k=1,2,\dots,n
, to finish this part of the proof with CLT, it remains to check the Lindeberg condition for CLT, which reduced to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(|\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})|\geq\tau\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}\right)}{\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}}\to 0\ {\rm as}\ n\to\infty

for all
\tau>0
. Note that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(|\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})|\geq\tau\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}\right)}{\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{4}}{\tau^{2}n\left(\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}\right)^{2}}.

Since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right)^{4}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}({\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v})\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right)^{4},

and

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{2}\left(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}^{2}\left(\mathscr{E}_{k}\times_{1}({\cal P}_{j}^{\perp}\mathbf{v})\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right)^{2},

with the fact that for a centered normal random variable
\xi
,
\mathbb{E}\xi^{4}=3\mathbb{E}^{2}\xi^{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{4}}{\tau^{2}n\left(\mathbb{E}\eta_{jk}(\mathbf{v})^{2}\right)^{2}}=\frac{1}{\tau^{2}n}\to 0,

and (
50
) follows.

D.2
Step 2.

Again, it suffices to consider the case
q=1
. We shall now argue that

\displaystyle\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{d_{1}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\right)}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d^{3/2}}{n^{3/2}}\right).

(51)

Write
\tilde{\Sigma}^{[1]}=\widehat{\Sigma}^{[1]}-\mathscr{I}
,
\tilde{\Sigma}^{[2]}=\widehat{\Sigma}^{[2]}-\mathscr{I}
, and let

\displaystyle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}=\tilde{\Sigma}^{[1]}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}),

and

\displaystyle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}=\tilde{\Sigma}^{[2]}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[1]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[1]},\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[1]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[1]}).

Since
\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}
is the leading eigenvector of

\widehat{\Sigma}^{[1]}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}),

it is also the leading eigenvector of

\tilde{\Sigma}^{[1]}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}),

so

\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}=\frac{\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|},

and similarly

\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}=\frac{\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}.

Write

\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}:=\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}-\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}-\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]},

where

\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}:={\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\left[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\right].

(52)

\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[2]}
and
\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}
are defined similarly so that

\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}=\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}+\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}+\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]},

\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}=\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}+\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}+\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[2]}.

We shall treat the three terms in
\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}
and
\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}
separately to show that

\displaystyle\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle=\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right),\ \langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle=\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right),

(53)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}+\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}\right\|=2\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}}\sqrt{\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{4}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(54)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}\right\|=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2d_{1}}{n}}\sqrt{\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{4}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(55)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}\right\|=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2d_{1}}{n}}\sqrt{\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{4}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}=O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right),\ \mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[2]}=O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

(56)

We first show that equation (
51
) follows from the bounds given in (
53
)-(
56
). We start with
\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|
. Combining (
53
)-(
56
), we have

\displaystyle\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\right\rangle^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}+\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}\right\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left[\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)\right]^{2}+\left[O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right),

(57)

where the second equality follows from (
53
), (
55
), (
56
), and the last equality holds because
d=o(n)
. Similarly we can derive that

\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|=\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left({d}/{n}\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}(\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}+\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]})+\left(\frac{1}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}+\left(\frac{1}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\right)}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right)\cdot O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\right)}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

(58)

where the second equality follows from (
54
), (
55
) and (
57
).

Moreover, (
56
), (
57
) imply that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[2]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right),

(59)

and (
53
) and (
57
) imply that

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}=2+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

(60)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}}\over\sqrt{\left(\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[2]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1\Bigg/\sqrt{1+\left(\frac{\frac{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[2]}}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}}{\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\|}+\frac{\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle}{\|\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\|}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1\Bigg/\sqrt{1+\left(\frac{2\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\right)}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)}{2+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1\Bigg/\sqrt{1+\left\{\left[\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\right)}+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)\right]\cdot\left[1+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)\right]\right\}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1\over\sqrt{1+\left[\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\right)}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{d_{1}}{n}\left(\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{4}}{\sigma_{j}^{4}}\right)}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d^{3/2}}{n^{3/2}}\right),

where the third equation follows from (
58
)-(
60
), and the last equation follows from the simple fact that

\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+(x+y)^{2}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+x^{2}}}\right|\leq\left|xy+\frac{1}{2}y^{2}\right|.

It now remains to show (
53
)-(
56
).

D.2.1
Equations (
54
) and (
55
).

Write

\mathbf{x}_{k}:=\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}.

If we choose an orthogonal basis of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
with
\mathbf{e}_{1}=\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}+\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]})\right\|=

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{k}\big(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathbf{x}_{1,k}\big)\right]\right\|=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{(-1),k}\big(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathbf{x}_{1,k}\big)\right\|,

in which
\mathbf{x}_{1,k}
