means the first entry of
\mathbf{x}_{k}
, while
\mathbf{x}_{(-1),k}
stands for all the other entries (having a dimension of
d_{1}-1
).

Observe that
\mathbf{x}_{(-1),k}
and
\big(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathbf{x}_{1,k}\big)
two independent group of i.i.d. random variables,
\mathbf{x}_{(-1),k}
follows distribution
N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}I_{d_{1}-1})
, and
\big(\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}+\mathbf{x}_{1,k}\big)
follows distribution
N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}+\sigma_{j}^{2})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[1]}+\mathbf{y}_{j}^{(1)[2]})\right\|=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{d_{1}-1}{n}}\sqrt{\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+\sigma_{0}^{4}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}-1}}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

(
55
) can be proven in a similar way.

D.2.2
Equation (
56
).

Recall that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Sigma}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{lk}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}\neq l_{2}}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{l_{2}k}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})-\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},

so by definition,

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{lk}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}\neq l_{2},l_{1}\neq j}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{l_{2}k}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle+{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\bigg[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\bigg]

\displaystyle+{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\bigg[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})-\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})+\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\bigg],

(61)

and we will deal with those six terms one by one.

The first term.

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{1}^{[1]}:=\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{lk}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{z}_{1}^{[1]}\right\|\leq\big(\delta^{(1)[2]}\big)^{2p-1}\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{lk}^{2}\right|=O_{p}\left[\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)^{2p-1}\right]=O_{p}\left[\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)^{3/2}\right].

(62)

The last two equality’s follow from (
44
), and
p\geq 2
. Because of the assumption
d=o(n)
, it implies

\left\|\mathbf{z}_{1}^{[1]}\right\|\leq O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

The second term.

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{2}^{[1]}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}\neq l_{2},l_{1}\neq j}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{l_{2}k}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}\neq l_{2},l_{1}\neq j,l_{2}\neq j}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{l_{2}k}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{2}}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}\neq j}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{j}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{jk}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\mathbf{u}_{l_{1}}^{(1)}.

Because

\left|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{l_{2}k}\right|=O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}),\ \forall l_{1}\neq l_{2},

we immediately have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{z}_{2}^{[1]}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big(\delta^{(1)[2]}\big)^{2p-1}\sum_{l_{1}\neq l_{2},l_{1}\neq j,l_{2}\neq j}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{l_{2}}\left|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{l_{2}k}\right|+\big(\delta^{(1)[2]}\big)^{p-1}\sum_{l_{1}\neq j}\sigma_{l_{1}}\sigma_{j}\left|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{l_{1}k}\theta_{jk}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})\cdot O_{p}\left[\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)^{2p-1}\right]+O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})\cdot\left[\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)^{p-1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\right).

(63)

The third term.

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{3}^{[1]}:=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{z}_{3}^{[1]}\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\sigma_{l}\left|\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right|\cdot\left|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{lk}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r\sigma_{1}(\delta^{(1)[2]})^{p-1}\cdot\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}^{[1]}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)^{p-1}\cdot O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right),

(64)

in which the second to last equality follows from (
44
) and (
43
).

The fourth term.

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{4}^{[1]}:=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l\neq j,l\in[r]}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle.

Similar to
\mathbf{z}_{3}^{[1]}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{z}_{4}^{[1]}\big\|\leq\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\cdot\left(\delta^{(1)[2]}\right)^{p}=O_{p}\left(\frac{d^{3/2}}{n^{3/2}}\right)=O_{p}\left(d\over n\right).

The fifth term.

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{5}^{[1]}:=

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\bigg[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\bigg].

we shall introduce the notation
\biguplus
, as the operation of expanding all the

\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}=\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}+\Delta_{j}^{(q)[2]},

and

\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}=\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}+\grave{\Delta}_{j}^{(1)[1]},

and then keep all the terms with at least one
\Delta_{j}^{(\cdot)[2]}
or
\grave{\Delta}_{j}^{(1)[1]}
in it. For instance, here, expanding

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

would result in
2^{2p-1}
terms, and we keep everything other than the term

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle\right).

Then we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{5}^{[1]}=

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\left\{\biguplus\Bigg[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle\Bigg]\right\}.

Then,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{z}_{5}^{[1]}\big\|\leq\left\|\biguplus\Bigg[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=1}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\Bigg]\right\|,

and for every term in
\biguplus
, its norm is bounded by

\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\cdot\delta^{(1)[2]}=O_{p}\big(\frac{d}{n}\big),

so that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{z}_{5}^{[1]}\big\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

The sixth term.

This term can be treated in a similar fashion as the last term.

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{6}^{[1]}:=

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\bigg[\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})-\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\mathscr{E}_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})+\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{k}-\mathscr{I}\bigg)(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\bigg(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{k}-\mathscr{I}\bigg)(\cdot,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)})\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}}^{\perp}\left\{\biguplus\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{k}-\mathscr{I}\bigg)(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\Bigg]\right\},

then,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{z}_{6}^{[1]}\big\|\leq\left\|\biguplus\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\mathscr{E}_{k}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{k}-\mathscr{I}\bigg)(\cdot,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\Bigg]\right\|,

and for every term in
\biguplus
, its norm is bounded by

\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\mathscr{E}}-\mathscr{I}\|\cdot\delta^{(1)[2]}=O_{p}\big(\frac{d}{n}\big),

so that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{z}_{6}^{[1]}\big\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

D.2.3
Equation (
53
)

We now show that

\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle=\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

It follows immediately, by symmetry, that

\displaystyle\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle=\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

By definition,

\displaystyle\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{jk}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l\neq j}\sigma_{l}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{jk}\theta_{lk}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}(\mathscr{E}_{k}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{k}-\mathscr{I})(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}).

(65)

All the terms except the first one will be bounded with similar techniques as we bound the six terms in
\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}
, and we omit some of the details.

The first term.

Observe that

1-\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\leq\big(\delta^{(1)[2]}\big)^{2},\ 1-\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)[1]}\rangle\leq\big(\delta^{(1)[2]}\big)^{2}.

Thus,

\sigma_{j}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{jk}^{2}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle=\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right).

The second term.

\displaystyle\sum_{l\neq j}\sigma_{l}\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\theta_{jk}\theta_{lk}\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})\cdot O_{p}\left[\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\right)^{2p-1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}).

The third term.

Observe that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}),

in which the first term is
O_{p}(1/\sqrt{n})
because
\theta_{jk}\mathscr{E}_{k},k\in[n/2]
are independent with
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}
, and the second term is bounded by

\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta,\mathscr{E}}\|\cdot\delta^{(1)[2]}=O_{p}\big(\frac{d}{n}\big),

so

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sigma_{j}\theta_{jk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\mathscr{E}_{k}(\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})=O_{p}\left(d\over n\right)+O_{p}\left(1\over\sqrt{n}\right)=O_{p}\left(d\over n\right).

The last equality follows from the assumption
d=o(n)
.

The fourth term.

Recall that
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}
is a function of the second half of the data, which is independent of the first half of the data, i.e., all the random variables with index
k\leq n/2
, so conditional on the second half of the data, for any
l\in[r]
,

\mathscr{E}_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\theta_{lk}

are a product of two independent
N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2})
and
N(0,1)
variables, and they are i.i.d across
k\leq n/2
.
So we have

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathscr{E}_{k}(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]})\sigma_{l}\theta_{lk}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}\rangle=O_{p}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}).

The fifth term.

Similar to the sixth term in
\mathbf{z}_{j}^{(1)[1]}
,

\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}(\mathscr{E}_{k}\otimes\mathscr{E}_{k}-\mathscr{I})(\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\dots,\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}).

Combining the five bounds above, we get

\langle\grave{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\rangle=\sigma_{j}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d}{n}\right)+O_{p}\left(1\over\sqrt{n}\right)=O_{p}\left(d\over n\right).

The last equality follows from the assumption
d=o(n)
.

D.3
Step 3.

Recall that

\widehat{b}_{j}^{(q)[1]}:=\left\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[1][1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[1][2]}\right\rangle-1,

\widehat{b}_{j}^{(q)[2]}:=\left\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[2][1]},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[2][2]}\right\rangle-1.

In this part, we use Lemma
8
to show that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[1]}-b_{j}^{(1)[1]}\right)\overset{p}{\to}0,

(66)

in which
b_{j}^{(1)[1]}
is defined in Lemma
3
.
Similarly
\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[2]}-b_{j}^{(1)[2]}\right)\overset{p}{\to}0
. Then, recall that by definition (
39
),

\displaystyle b_{j}^{(1)}=\frac{\left\|\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}+\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\right\|}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[1]}}+\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{(1)[2]}}}-1,

and by definition (
13
),

\displaystyle\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)}=\frac{\left\|\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[1]}+\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q),[2]}\right\|}{\sqrt{1+\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[1]}}+\sqrt{1+\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[2]}}}-1,

we have that (
42
) follows from (
66
) and the fact that
\left\|\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[1]}+\check{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1),[2]}\right\|\leq 2
. Now we turn our attention to (
66
).

By Lemma
4
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\|\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}-M_{j}^{[1]}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}}\bigvee\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}{(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)n},

on events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
. (
M_{j}^{[1]}
,
\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}
and events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
are defined at the beginning of the proof for Lemma
3
.) The last inequality holds because of (
98
) and (
94
). Remember that
\bar{g}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}
(inequality (
96
)), so with assumption
d=o(n)
, we have that for large enough
n
,
\bar{\mathbb{E}}\|\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}-M_{j}^{[1]}\|\leq\frac{1}{4}\bar{g}_{1}
, i.e., if we let
\gamma=\frac{1}{4}
, then

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\|\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}-M_{j}^{[1]}\|\leq\frac{(1-2\gamma)\bar{g}_{1}}{2}.

1+b_{j}^{(1)[1]}\geq 2\gamma
is also satisfied for large enough
n
by inequality (
99
).

Let
t=\left(\frac{n}{2r(M_{j}^{[1]})}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}
. First note that on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
,

\displaystyle t\geq\left(\frac{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}{2(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},

by inequality (
98
), with assumption
d=o(n)
, we have
t\geq 1
for large enough
n
.

Let
D:=D_{\frac{1}{4}}
as in Lemma
8
. We have

\displaystyle D\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\left(\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\leq D(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\left(n^{-\frac{2}{3}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{8}\bar{g}_{1}

for large enough
n
.

So all the conditions in Lemma
8
are satisfied with
\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}
and
M_{j}^{[1]}
, conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{k},k=n/2+1,\dots,n\}
under events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, for large enough
n
.

Observe that conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{k},k=n/2+1,\dots,n\}
,

\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[1]}
and
b_{j}^{(1)[1]}
are just defined as the
\widehat{b}_{1}
and
b_{1}
[defined in (
78
) and (
79
)] corresponding to
M_{j}^{[1]}
. Now we can apply Lemma
8
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left|\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[1]}-b_{j}^{(1)[1]}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D\sqrt{n}\frac{\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D\frac{\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigvee\frac{1}{r(M_{j}^{[1]})^{\frac{1}{3}}n^{\frac{1}{6}}}\bigvee\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{r(M_{j}^{[1]})^{\frac{1}{6}}n^{\frac{1}{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D\frac{\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigvee\frac{1}{n^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+1}{\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)^{2}\left[\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigvee\frac{1}{n^{\frac{1}{6}}}\ \right]

(67)

conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{k},k=n/2+1,\dots,n\}
under events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, for large enough
n
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
. The last inequality holds because of (
94
), (
96
) and (
98
).

Since on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
, (
67
) holds with probability at least
1-e^{-t}\geq 1-\exp\left[-\left(\frac{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}{2(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right]
, we have that on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, (
67
) also holds with probability at least
1-\exp\left[-\left(\frac{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}{2(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right]
. Combine with assumption
\frac{d}{n}\to 0
, we have that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[1]}-b_{j}^{(1)[1]}\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{B}_{n})\overset{p}{\to}0,

which leads to

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\widehat{b}_{j}^{(1)[1]}-b_{j}^{(1)[1]}\right)\overset{p}{\to}0.

Appendix E
Proof of Lemma
3

The proof of Lemma
3
relies heavily on the techniques and results from
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2014
which we will review first.

E.1
Preliminaries

Let
\mathbb{H}
be a Hilbert space and
M:\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{H}
be a compact symmetric nonnegative definite operator. It is well known that the following spectral representation holds

\displaystyle M=\sum_{r>1}\mu_{r}{\cal P}_{r}

with distinct non-zero eigenvalues
\mu_{r}
arranged in decreasing order
\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}>\dots\geq 0
, and
{\cal P}_{r}
are the corresponding spectral projectors. The effective rank of
M
is defined as

\displaystyle r(M):=\frac{{\rm tr}(M)}{\|M\|}.

We will use in particular the results from
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2014
for the estimation of
{\cal P}_{1}
, in the case where

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{1}=\mathbf{u}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{1},

i.e., estimating the leading eigenvector in the case that the leading eigenvalue is an isolated simple eigenvalue.
Let
Y_{1},Y_{2},\dots,Y_{n}
be i.i.d. centered Gaussian random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
with covariance
M=\mathbb{E}(Y\otimes Y)
. Let

\displaystyle\widehat{M}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}Y_{k}\otimes Y_{k}

be the sample covariance matrix based on the observations
(Y_{1},Y_{2},\dots,Y_{n})
. The following lemma is a restatement of Theorem 1 from
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2014
.

Lemma 4
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{M}-M\|\asymp\|M\|\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M)}{n}}\bigvee\frac{r(M)}{n}\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{M}-M\|^{2}\asymp\|M\|^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M)}{n}}\bigvee\frac{r(M)}{n}\right)^{2}.

Let
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
be the leading eigenvector of
\widehat{M}
. Without loss of generality, to make the linear form of
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
well-defined, we always assume that
\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1}\rangle\geq 0
. Denote
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}:=\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
. Define
\bar{g}_{1}:=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
, the spectral gap of
\mu_{1}
, and write

\displaystyle{\cal C}_{1}=\sum_{s\neq 1}\frac{1}{\mu_{1}-\mu_{s}}{\cal P}_{s},

(68)

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{1}

\displaystyle:={\cal C}_{1}(\widehat{M}-M){\cal P}_{1}+{\cal P}_{1}(\widehat{M}-M){\cal C}_{1}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}({\cal C}_{1}Y_{j}\otimes{\cal P}_{1}Y_{j}+{\cal P}_{1}Y_{j}\otimes{\cal C}_{1}Y_{j}),

(69)

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}-{\cal P}_{1}-{\cal L}_{1},

(70)

and the remainder in terms of operator

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{1}:=\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}-\mathbb{E}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}-{\cal L}_{1}.

(71)

Note that
\mathbb{E}{\cal L}_{1}=0
, so
{\cal R}_{1}={\cal S}_{1}-\mathbb{E}{\cal S}_{1}
. As in the proof of Theorem
4.4
, we use calligraphic capital letters on
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}
,
{\cal P}_{1}
,
{\cal C}_{1}
,
{\cal L}_{1}
,
{\cal S}_{1}
and
{\cal R}_{1}
to signify that they are matrices. Rephrasing Lemma 1 in
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2014
, we have

Lemma 5
.

\displaystyle\|{\cal S}_{1}\|\leq 14\left(\frac{\|\widehat{M}-M\|}{\bar{g}_{1}}\right)^{2}

(72)

Combine Lemma
5
and
4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|{\cal S}_{1}\|\leq C\frac{\|M\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M)}{n}}\bigvee\frac{r(M)}{n}\right)^{2},

(73)

where
C
is a universal constant. Restating Theorems 3 and 4 of
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2014
, we get

Lemma 6
.

Let
t>1
and suppose that, for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
and a sufficiently large constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{M}-M\|+C\|M\|\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\leq\frac{1-\gamma}{1+\gamma}\frac{\bar{g}_{1}}{2}.

(74)

Then there exists a constant
D_{\gamma}>0
such that, for all
u,v\in\mathbb{H}
, the following bound holds with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
:

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}-\mathbb{E}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}-{\cal L}_{1})u,v\big\rangle\right|\leq D_{\gamma}\frac{\|M\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M)}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\|u\|\|v\|.

(75)

Lemma 7
.

Suppose that for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
and a sufficiently large constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{M}-M\|+C\|M\|\frac{\log n}{n}\leq(1-\gamma)\frac{\bar{g}_{1}}{2}.

(76)

Then, there exists a constant
D_{\gamma}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}-{\cal P}_{1}-{\cal P}_{1}\mathbb{E}({\cal S}_{1}){\cal P}_{1}\|\leq D_{\gamma}\frac{\|M\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M)}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right).

(77)

With
{\cal S}_{1}
defined, we can define a critical quantity that characterizes the bias of
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}
:

\displaystyle b_{1}:=\left\langle\mathbb{E}({\cal S}_{1})\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1}\right\rangle.

(78)

Note that
\mathbb{E}{\cal L}_{1}=0
and
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{1}:=\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
, in which
\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1}\rangle\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle b_{1}=\left\langle\mathbb{E}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1})\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1}\right\rangle-1,

so
-1\leq b_{1}\leq 0
.

There is a way to estimate
b_{1}
. Suppose we divide the sample
(Y_{1},Y_{2},\dots,Y_{n})
into two subsamples of sample size
\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor
each. Let
\check{M}
be the sample covariance based on the first subsample and
\check{M}^{\prime}
be the sample covariance based on the second subsample. Denote by
\check{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
the leading eigenvector of
\check{M}
and by
\check{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\prime}
the leading eigenvector of
\check{M}^{\prime}
. Assume that their signs are chosen in such a way that
\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\check{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\geq 0
. Define

\displaystyle\widehat{b}_{1}:=\langle\check{\mathbf{u}}_{1},\check{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\prime}\rangle-1.

(79)

The following lemma, a restatement of Proposition 3 from
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2014
, provides a concentration inequality of
|\widehat{b}_{1}-b_{1}|
.

Lemma 8
.

Let
t\geq 1
and
\gamma\in(0,1/2)
. There exists a constant
D_{\gamma}>0
such that, if

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{M}-M\|\leq\frac{(1-2\gamma)\bar{g}_{1}}{2},\ 1+b_{1}\geq 2\gamma

(80)

and

\displaystyle D_{\gamma}\|M\|\left(\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\leq\frac{\gamma\bar{g}_{1}}{2},

(81)

then with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{b}_{1}-b_{1}|\leq D_{\gamma}\frac{\|M\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M)}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}.

(82)

Proof outline.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\|\mathbf{u}\|,\|\mathbf{v}\|=1
throughout the proof. Write

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{j}:=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left({\cal C}_{j}y_{jk}\otimes{\cal P}_{j}y_{jk}+{\cal P}_{j}y_{jk}\otimes{\cal C}_{j}y_{jk}\right),

(83)

then

\displaystyle\langle{\cal L}_{j}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle+\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\right].

(84)

Now Lemma
3
is equivalent to: there exists universal constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|b_{j}^{(q)[1]}|\leq C\frac{d}{n})\to 1,

(85)

and for
\forall\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{H}
,
\|\mathbf{u}\|,\|\mathbf{v}\|\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\bigg\langle\left[\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}({\cal S}^{[1]}){\cal P}_{j}-{\cal L}_{j}\right]\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\bigg\rangle\overset{p}{\to}0.

(86)

We separate the proof for (
86
) into three parts:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\big\langle(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-\bar{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal L}^{[1]})u,v\big\rangle\overset{p}{\to}0,

(87)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\left\|\bar{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}(S^{[1]}){\cal P}_{j}\right\|\overset{p}{\to}0,

(88)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\big\langle({\cal L}^{[1]}-{\cal L}_{j})u,v\big\rangle\overset{p}{\to}0.

(89)

We will first prove some preliminary bounds and (
85
), and then come back to (
87
), (
88
) and (
89
) to complete the proof.

E.2
Proof for (
85
).

Upper bounds (
16
) and (
17
) imply that

\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}
satisfies the following conditions:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\|\leq a_{n},\ \forall q\in[p]\right)=1,

(90)

where
a_{n}
is a numeric sequence such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}a_{n}=0
, and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{l\neq j}\left|\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right|\leq A,\ \forall q\in[p]\right)=1,

(91)

where
A
is a numeric constant. Define events

\mathcal{B}_{n}:=\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\|\leq a_{n},\ \|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\|\leq a_{n},\ \forall q\in[p]\right\}.

By (
90
) and (
91
),
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}_{n})=1
.
Note that event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
belongs to the sigma field of
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
, so it suffices to treat
\bar{\mathbb{P}}(\cdot)
and
\bar{\mathbb{E}}(\cdot)
as conditional on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
. Since events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
satisfy
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}_{n})=1
, for any sequence of random variables
Z_{n}
, to prove
Z_{n}\overset{p}{\to}0
, we only need to show
Z_{n}\cdot\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{B}_{n})\overset{p}{\to}0
, where
\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{B}_{n})
is the indicator function of event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
. We will use this technique extensively.

We only need to prove for the case
q=1
. From now on till the end of this proof, for simplicity of notations, we denote
{\cal P}_{j}={\cal P}_{j}^{(1)}
,
{\cal C}_{j}={\cal C}_{j}^{(1)}
,
\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}=\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}^{(1)}
,

M_{j}^{[1]}=M^{(1)[1]}
,
{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}={\cal C}_{j}^{(1)[1]}
,
{\cal L}_{j}^{[1]}={\cal L}_{j}^{(1)[1]}
,
{\cal S}_{j}^{[1]}={\cal S}_{j}^{(1)[1]}
.
Furthermore, write

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{-}=I_{d_{q}}-\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}

(92)

Note that
{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]},{\cal L}_{j}^{[1]},{\cal S}_{j}^{[1]},R^{[1]}
are the
{\cal C}_{1},{\cal L}_{1},{\cal S}_{1},{\cal R}_{1}
[defined in (
68
), (
69
), (
70
), (
71
)] corresponding to our covariance matrix
M_{j}^{[1]}
. Conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{k},k=n/2+1,\dots,n\}
,

\displaystyle z_{jk}:=\mathscr{X}_{k}\times_{2}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(1)[2]}\dots\times_{p}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]},k=1,\dots,n/2

has covariance matrix
M_{j}^{[1]}
, and
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[1]}
is the leading eigenvector of the sample covariance matrix

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}z_{jk}\otimes z_{jk}.

(93)

We first prove some inequalities for
\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|
,
\bar{g}_{1}
the first spectral gap of
M_{j}^{[1]}
, and
r(M_{j}^{[1]})
the effective rank of
M_{j}^{[1]}
, under event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
. These inequalities will be used extensively throughout the proof.

Under
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, since
\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\|\leq a_{n}
, we have
(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}\leq\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}\leq\sigma_{j}^{2}
. Moreover, because
\max_{l\neq j}\left|\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(2)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\rangle\right|\leq A
is bounded, and
\left|\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(2)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{1-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\rangle^{2}}\leq a_{n}
, we have
\max_{l\neq j}\{\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\}\leq Aa_{n}
. Since
\lim_{n\to\infty}a_{n}=0
, for large enough
n
, we have
(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}>Aa_{n}
. So the leading eigenvector of
M_{j}^{[1]}
is
\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}
, with corresponding eigenvalue
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}+1
. So for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1\leq(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1\leq\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\leq\sigma_{j}^{2}+1,

(94)

and the spectral gap for the leading eigenvalue of
M_{j}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{1}\geq(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}-Aa_{n}.

(95)

Since
\lim_{n\to\infty}a_{n}=0
, we have for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}.

(96)

By definition,

\displaystyle r(M_{j}^{[1]})=\frac{{\rm tr}(M_{j}^{[1]})}{\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|}.

(97)

Combine with (
94
), and

\displaystyle{\rm tr}(M_{j}^{[1]})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\neq j}\sigma_{l}^{2}\left(\prod_{q=2}^{p}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}\rangle\right)^{2}+\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}+d_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{l\neq j}\left|\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(2)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\rangle\right|+\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Aa_{n}+\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}

where the first inequality is by Cauchy inequality. So for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\frac{d_{1}}{\sigma_{j}^{2}+1}\leq r(M_{j}^{[1]})\leq\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1}.

(98)

Combine inequalities (
73
) from Lemma
6
with bounds (
94
), (
96
) and (
98
), we have that under event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\left|b_{j}^{(1)[1]}\right|\leq\left\|\bar{\mathbb{E}}({\cal S}_{j}^{[1]})\right\|\leq\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\cal S}_{j}^{[1]}\right\|\leq C\frac{(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)^{2}}{\frac{1}{4}\sigma_{j}^{4}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}}\bigvee\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}\right)^{2},

(99)

since we treat
\sigma_{j}
as fixed, the right hand side is bounded by
C\left(\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\bigvee\frac{d}{n}\right)^{2}\leq C\frac{d}{n}
.
Recall that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}_{n})=1
, we have
\mathbb{P}(|b_{j}^{(1)[1]}|\leq C\frac{d}{n})\to 1
as
n\to\infty
.

E.3
Proof for (
87
).

In this part, we will use Lemma
6
to show that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\big\langle(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-\bar{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal L}_{j}^{[1]})\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\big\rangle\overset{p}{\to}0.

Let
t=\left(\frac{n}{2r(M_{j}^{[1]})}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}
. First note that on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, by inequality (
98
),

\displaystyle t\geq\left(\frac{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}{2(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\geq 1,

for large enough
n
. By Lemma
4
,

\displaystyle\delta_{n}(t)

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathbb{E}}\|\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}-M_{j}^{[1]}\|+C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}}\bigvee\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}\right)+C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\left[\frac{4(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}{(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)n}+\sqrt{\frac{2\left(\frac{n}{2r(M_{j}^{[1]})}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}}{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\left[\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)n}+n^{-\frac{1}{3}}\right],

on events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
. The last inequality holds because of (
98
) and (
94
). Recall that
\bar{g}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}
. We have that for large enough
n
,
\delta_{n}(t)\leq\frac{1}{6}\bar{g}_{1}
.

Now, by Lemma
6
with
t=\left(\frac{n}{2r(M_{j}^{[1]})}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}
and
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}
, there exists a constant
D:=D_{\frac{1}{2}}
such that on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}\geq 1-\exp\left[-\left(\frac{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}{2(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right]
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\left|\big\langle(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-\bar{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal L}_{j}^{[1]})\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\frac{\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D\frac{\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigvee\frac{1}{r(M_{j}^{[1]})^{\frac{1}{3}}(n/2)^{\frac{1}{6}}}\ \right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4D\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+1}{\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)^{2}\left[\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigvee\frac{1}{n^{\frac{1}{6}}}\ \right],

(100)

conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{k},k=n/2+1,\dots,n\}
under events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, for large enough
n
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
. The last inequality holds because of (
94
), (
96
) and (
98
).

Since on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
, (
100
) holds with probability at least
1-\exp\left[-\left(\frac{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}{2(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right]
, we have that on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, (
100
) also holds with probability at least
1-\exp\left[-\left(\frac{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}{2(\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\right]
. Combining with the assumption that
{d}/{n}\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\left|\big\langle(\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-\bar{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal L}_{j}^{[1]})\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\big\rangle\right|\cdot\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{B}_{n})\overset{p}{\to}0.

E.4
Proof for (
88
).

In this part, we will use Lemma
7
to show that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\left\|\bar{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}({\cal S}_{j}^{[1]}){\cal P}_{j}\right\|\overset{p}{\to}0.

By Lemma
4
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\|\widehat{M}_{j}^{[1]}-M_{j}^{[1]}\|+C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\frac{\log n}{n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}}\bigvee\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}\right)+C\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|\frac{\log n}{n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\left[\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)n}+\frac{\log n}{n}\right],

on events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
. The last inequality holds because of (
98
) and (
94
). Remember that
\bar{g}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}
(inequality
96
), since
d=o(n)
, we have that for large enough
n
,
\delta_{n}(t)\leq\frac{1}{4}\bar{g}_{1}
.

Now, by Lemma
7
, with
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}
, there exists a constant
D:=D_{\frac{1}{2}}
such that on the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}}\left\|\bar{\mathbb{E}}\widehat{{\cal P}}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}-{\cal P}_{j}\bar{\mathbb{E}}({\cal S}_{j}^{[1]}){\cal P}_{j}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D\sqrt{n/2}\frac{\|M_{j}^{[1]}\|^{2}}{\bar{g}_{1}^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n/2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r(M_{j}^{[1]})}{n}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4D\left(\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+1}{\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)^{2}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{j}^{2}+d_{1}+1}{n(\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)}}\bigvee\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right],

(101)

for large enough
n
. The last inequality holds because of (
94
), (
96
) and (
98
). Equation (
88
) then follows.

E.5
Proof for (
89
).

Note that for
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{H}
,

\displaystyle\langle{\cal L}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle+\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{v}\rangle\right],

(102)

and remember that
\langle L_{j}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\rangle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle+\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\right]
, so to prove (
89
), we only need to show: for
\forall\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{H}
,
\|\mathbf{u}\|,\|\mathbf{v}\|\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]\overset{p}{\to}0.

(103)

We further break the proof for (
103
) into three steps.

Step 1.

\displaystyle\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle

(104)

but conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
,
\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle
and
\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle
,
k=1,\dots,n
are mean-
0
Gaussian random variables and uncorrelated hence independent. So
\bar{\mathbb{E}}(\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle)=0
. Moreover, direct calculation gives us

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\mathscr{X}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\dots\times_{p}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}-\mathscr{X}_{k}\times_{1}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)}\times_{2}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\dots\times_{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{r}\left(1-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}+\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\otimes\dots\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{r}\left(1-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}+\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}\left(2-2\prod_{q=2}^{r}\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{4}a_{n}^{4}\right)^{p-1}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}

(105)

since
\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\|\leq a_{n}
which implies
1-\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]},\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle\leq\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}
.
Also we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]^{2}=\langle{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}M_{j}^{[1]}{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}\rangle,

(106)

where

\displaystyle{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}M_{j}^{[1]}{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}:=\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}{\left(\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\right)^{2}}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{4}}{\cal P}_{-}.

(107)

As already proven,
(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}\leq\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}
, and
\max_{l\neq j}\{\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\}\leq Aa_{n}\to 0
, and for large enough
n
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}
, so for large enough
n
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}M_{j}^{[1]}{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\left[(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}\right]^{4}}.

(108)

Thus,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left[(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}\right]^{4}}\left[\sigma_{j}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{4}a_{n}^{4}\right)^{p-1}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right].

(109)

Step 2.

\displaystyle\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle

(110)

but conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
,
\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle
and
\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle
,
k=1,\dots,n
are mean-
0
Gaussian random variables and uncorrelated hence independent. So
\bar{\mathbb{E}}(\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle)=0
.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\neq j}\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\rangle^{2}\prod_{q=2}^{r}\left(\langle\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]},\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(q)}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\right\|^{2}\cdot\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\otimes\dots\otimes\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(p)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(p)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\right\|^{2}\max_{l\neq j}\left|\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(2)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\rangle\right|\sqrt{1-\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(2)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\rangle^{2}}+\left\|{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\right\|^{2}\left(2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right)

(111)

Since

\displaystyle{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}=\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}}{\cal P}_{-},

(112)

As already proven, for large enough
n
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}
, so for large enough
n
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\right\|\leq\frac{2}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}.

(113)

Because
\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(q)[2]}-\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(q)}\|\leq a_{n}
, and
\max_{l\neq j}\left|\sigma_{l}^{2}\langle\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(2)},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{(2)[2]}\rangle\right|\leq A
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left[Aa_{n}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right]

(114)

and
E\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}=(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},v\rangle^{2}
, so

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk}-y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]^{2}\leq(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\frac{2}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left[Aa_{n}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right]

(115)

Step 3.

\displaystyle\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},({\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}-{\cal C}_{j})\mathbf{u}\rangle

(116)

but conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
,
\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle
and
\langle y_{jk},({\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}-{\cal C}_{j})\mathbf{u}\rangle
,
k=1,\dots,n
are mean-
0
Gaussian random variables and uncorrelated hence independent. So
\bar{\mathbb{E}}(\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},({\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}-{\cal C}_{j})\mathbf{u}\rangle)=0
.

Moreover,

\displaystyle{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}-{\cal C}_{j}=\sum_{l\neq j}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}+\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right){\cal P}_{-}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left(I_{\mathbb{H}}-I_{d_{1}}\right)

(117)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\langle y_{jk},({\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}-{\cal C}_{j})\mathbf{u}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\sum_{l\neq j}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}+\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right){\cal P}_{-}\right]\mathbf{u}\right\|^{2}

(118)

but as already proven,
(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}\leq\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}
, and
\max_{l\neq j}\{\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\}\leq Aa_{n}\to 0
, and for large enough
n
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}
, so for large enough
n
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{l\neq j}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{l}^{(1)}+\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right){\cal P}_{-}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{l\neq j}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)\bigvee\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{l\neq j}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{l}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}-Aa_{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}

(119)

and
E\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle^{2}=(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}^{(1)},v\rangle^{2}
,
so

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left[\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},({\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}-{\cal C}_{j})\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]^{2}\leq(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\left(\frac{1}{(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}-Aa_{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right).

(120)

Combining (
109
), (
115
), (
120
), we have that on events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left[(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}\right]^{4}}\left[\sigma_{j}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{4}a_{n}^{4}\right)^{p-1}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right]

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\frac{2}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left[Aa_{n}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right]+(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\left(\frac{1}{(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}-Aa_{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right).

Note that conditional on
\{\mathscr{X}_{n/2+1},\mathscr{X}_{n/2+2},\dots,\mathscr{X}_{n}\}
,

\displaystyle\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle

are independent with each other, so

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left[(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}\right]^{4}}\left[\sigma_{j}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{4}a_{n}^{4}\right)^{p-1}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right]

\displaystyle+(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\frac{2}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\left[Aa_{n}+2-2\left(1-\frac{1}{2}a_{n}^{2}\right)^{p-1}\right]+(\sigma_{j}^{2}+1)\left(\frac{1}{(1-a_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sigma_{j}^{2}-Aa_{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}\right),

on events
\mathcal{B}_{n}
, for large enough
n
. Combine with
\lim_{n}a_{n}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{B}_{n})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\bar{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]\right)^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{B}_{n})\right\}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\ 0.

So

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{n/2}\left[\langle z_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle z_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}^{[1]}\mathbf{u}\rangle-\langle y_{jk},{\cal P}_{j}\mathbf{v}\rangle\langle y_{jk},{\cal C}_{j}\mathbf{u}\rangle\right]\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}(\mathcal{B}_{n})\overset{p}{\to}0,

thus proving (
103
), and (
89
) follows.
